# Changelog

Related: [[Decisions]] · [[Bugs]] · [[Features]]

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file, in [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) format.

## [Unreleased]

- **2026-08-26**: **Move-out, rebuilt around the exit that actually happens.** The owner flow was five sequential forms walked identically whether the exit was disputed with damages or the ordinary case — course ended, tenant already gone, room fine. Now **two lanes over the same state machine** ([[Decisions#ADR-122|ADR-122]]): `POST /api/move-out/quick-exit` closes a clean exit in one action, while anything needing a *judgement* (damages to assess) or carrying a *disagreement* (an open dispute) still walks the staged flow. Money moving does **not** force the long path — a deposit refund or unpaid dues are normal at move-out, and the one screen takes a payment mode inline. Every hop still goes through the same public method and the same `assertTransition()`, so a one-tap exit has exactly the audit trail of a five-tap one. **Three fixes matter more than the speed.** **Completing a move-out silently wrote off every outstanding obligation** — `bulkWaiveInTx` ran unconditionally, behind a button labelled *"Confirm Refund & Complete"*, on a screen that had just displayed *Owed by tenant ₹25,000*: the owner tapped *refund* and forgave ₹25,000 owed to them, unasked and untold. `duesDisposition` now defaults to **`RECOVERABLE`** — the tenancy closes, the debt does not — and `WAIVE` says *"Write off ₹25,000 & close"* in words. This was nearly free: `reminder-service` and `rent-generation-service` both filter `status = "ACTIVE"`, so a former tenant accrues no late fee, no reminder and no new rent, while `payment-service` has no status gate and can still take their payment. **The owner entered deductions blind** — the inspection form asked for damage amounts with no dues, deposit or balance on screen, though `settlement_preview` was already attached at every non-terminal status; the money is now pinned to every step. **And the tenant was deleted from the app without a word** — a `FORMER_TENANT` failed `hasLiveTenancy` and was redirected to `/discover`, dashboard tabs gone, their own settlement record unreachable, and the redirect fired at `vacate`, a whole step *before* the money settled. Tenancy is now three states: `EXITING` keeps the dashboard read-only until the settlement closes, and `EXITED` lands on a farewell screen that says what happened, shows the receipt, and only then offers Discover. One-tap exits refuse to close an outcome the owner did not see: the server recomputes the settlement and rejects with `409 STALE_PREVIEW` if it moved. `ExitWorkflowSection.tsx` is deleted — a second copy of this pipeline that had drifted into letting the owner hand-overwrite the settlement amount. **Not exercised against a real database or tenant** (`DATABASE_URL_TEST` still unprovisioned); verified by typecheck, 63 new pure tests and the frontend build. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-26**: **Discover's Reviews section restyled toward Airbnb's own review UI, Amenities dropped as a rating question, and a hostel's first review now earns a real score.** Three related changes to the same section. **The visual rebuild** (merged as PR #35) split the 516-line `ReviewsSection.tsx` into `ReviewsScoreSummary.tsx` (big trust score, "Resident Favourite" pill, a horizontal 5-to-1 star distribution using `summary.distribution` — fetched since [[Decisions#ADR-115|ADR-115]] but never actually rendered until now — and the category grid), `ReviewsCard.tsx` (a real date line, independent per-card "Show more" truncation, a two-column desktop / one-column mobile grid) and `ReviewsWriteForm.tsx` (the resident's own review now renders as a compact status strip above the grid rather than a full card, so it can never read as review #1), plus a small `reviewCategoryMeta.ts` and a pure, unit-tested `reviewsPreview.ts` for the show-all/collapse logic. Rebuilt once already, from scratch, after discovering the first pass was written against a stale local copy of `ReviewsSection.tsx` that predated [[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]] — shipping it as-is would have reintroduced the locked "sign in to review" box ADR-102 removed. The second, correct pass was checked line-by-line against the real `origin/main` baseline before merging. **Amenities dropped** as one of the eight rating categories (down to seven: Cleanliness, Maintenance, Food, Room Comfort, Staff & Management, Safety, Wi-Fi), at the product owner's explicit call. `REVIEW_CATEGORIES` (`review-summary.ts`) is the single source of truth, so removing one entry updated the write form, the category grid, admin insights and highlight derivation together, with no other call site to touch. `rating_amenities` stays in the schema unused rather than triggering another production migration — the `stay_months`/migration-076 incident earlier the same day was still fresh reason enough to avoid an unforced one. **[[Decisions#ADR-121|ADR-121]] lowered `MIN_REVIEWS_FOR_AVERAGE` from 3 to 1** — a hostel's very first published review now shows the full score/distribution/category-grid view instead of "Too few reviews to average yet," the tradeoff (a single review is not a statistically trustworthy average) explicitly accepted rather than overlooked; `isResidentFavourite()`'s separate `count >= 5` floor is untouched. The category-grid tiles were also reshaped mid-session to match Airbnb's own layout (label, then number, then a small icon, stacked per category, not the original icon-circle-plus-row) and made desktop-only (`hidden sm:flex`) — the content column here is far narrower than Airbnb's full-bleed desktop page even at its widest, and a phone screen has no room for a multi-category row at all; every viewport still gets the Overall rating block. **Verified:** backend pure suite 23/23 for `review-summary.test.ts` at each step (including the updated 7-category and 1-review assertions), full pure suite 889/891 (the 2 failures are the pre-existing, unrelated `agreement-requirement.test.ts` baseline), `check:architecture` clean, new `reviewsPreview.test.ts` 6/6, `tsc --noEmit` showing the same 49 pre-existing/unrelated errors throughout (zero new ones at any step). Production confirmed live for the redesign + Amenities removal (`/api/health` build commit matched the merge commit; the live reviews endpoint returned exactly 7 categories) and visually confirmed by screenshot (the new date line and verified-badge text rendering on a real published review). **Not yet visually confirmed:** the score/distribution/category-grid block itself, the desktop-only category-tile reshape, or the 1-review-averages change — none had rendered in front of anyone by the time this entry was written, only proven by tests/types/API responses. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]] (the `stay_months` production migration-drift incident earlier the same day), [[Decisions#ADR-115|ADR-115]], [[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]].
- **2026-08-26**: **People own their own details, and can finally leave.** Two opposite gaps. **Changing your own phone or email needed your hostel owner's permission** — `GOVERNED_PROFILE_FIELDS` routed both through `change_requests` and an owner review queue holding **0 rows, ever**: never an approval, only ever a wall, and it locked residents out of the very number the product's WhatsApp messages go to. **And there was no way to delete an account** — no endpoint, no UI, nothing named anything close, in either app. Now **every profile field saves directly**; the reason box, the "awaiting owner approval" banners and the padlock glyphs are gone. **A changed phone still has to be proved**, by a code to the *new* number over WhatsApp, reusing `send-phone-otp`/`verify-phone-otp` and the [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]] trust rule — a number we already reached needs nothing, a number you are changing to needs a code — and where WhatsApp cannot deliver ([[Decisions#ADR-034|ADR-034]]) the edit saves anyway rather than waiting for a code that is not coming. **Email is proved the same way**, by a code to the new address — there was no email OTP leg, and rather than a hand-applied migration for a new table the codes live in **Redis** with a 10-minute TTL, which is the right store for a secret meant to expire anyway. **Three owner gates turned out to be in the service, not one:** beyond `GOVERNED_FIELDS` rejecting those fields with "require owner approval", an `allow_tenant_edits` preference let an owner **switch off a resident's ability to edit their own profile at all**. Both gone; `assertCapability(…, "EDIT_PROFILE")` stays, because it is a move-out *state* rule rather than a permission. The proof is re-checked on the PATCH itself, so skipping the verify call skips nothing, and unchanged values are never re-proved. The request system is **deleted, not unwired** — tenant route, owner approve/reject routes, the `owner-profile-requests` feature, its route and the owner Alerts card; the shared `change_requests` table and the live owner-proposes-tenant-approves facade are untouched. **Closing an account** is a new `POST /api/profile/close-account` plus a ceremony built on **friction made of information, not obstruction**: what you lose stated in your own numbers, what we cannot delete and why, and the smaller fix where one honestly exists — `retentionOffer` returns `null` for most reasons, because someone who has moved home will not be argued out of it and pretending otherwise is what makes a cancellation flow feel like a trap. A reason is required, the note is not. The account is **anonymised, not erased** (a hostel's ledger is not solely the leaver's to rewrite), and since **nothing in the login path reads `is_active`** the real lock is deleting the Supabase auth user plus a Redis revocation marker. Blockers — dues, then a settlement in flight, then a live tenancy — are checked server-side and shown one at a time. **No migration**: `system_event_logs.metadata` is jsonb and `profiles.is_active` already existed. Separately, the Help Centre and profile hub had already drifted apart (36px icons in `px-4 py-3.5` against 30px in `px-3.5 py-3`, different header padding), so both now render one vocabulary from `features/stayo-ui/ListSection.tsx` — [[Decisions#ADR-120|ADR-120]]. **Verified:** frontend 1396/1396 (+19), `tsc --noEmit` clean in both apps, production build, architecture and branding checks passing. **Not verified — and this matters:** closure is **irreversible and has never been run against a real account**; the phone-OTP edit path has not been driven end to end with a real code. **Copy and UI left over from the approval era, swept in the same change:** the Personal information screen still opened with a **"Request a correction"** button and the subtitle "Your identity on file" (now "Edit details" / "Yours to change, any time"); Academic and Work details said "Request an update"; and `profileDetailConfigs.ts` — **imported by nothing** — held a "Request a correction" button whose entire implementation was `stayoToast.success('Correction request sent to warden')`, a success message for an action that never happened. Deleted. The edit screen itself was tidied while open: a document row offered **"Re-upload" for a document that was never uploaded** (now Upload/Replace) and rendered "Not uploaded" in a monospace face meant for document numbers; empty fields had no placeholders and optional ones were unmarked; the `<select>` used the browser's arrow; a native date input showed an Indian birthday as **`05/21/2006`** in a US-defaulted browser, so the value is now restated as "21 May 2006" beneath rather than fighting a control that cannot be styled; the disabled Save button read as broken rather than as *nothing to save yet*, and now names what it will do ("Save 2 changes"); and **fields needing a code now say so before Save, not after** — discovering the cost only once you have committed is how a form feels like a trap. The panel moved off `bg-background` onto Stayo's graph-paper ground with a solid header and footer, matching every other screen. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Decisions#ADR-119|ADR-119]].

- **2026-08-26**: **The profile hub is one screen instead of three, and tells you what is missing rather than what you already know.** It had grown to ~2,000px under **8 uppercase headings** for 12 destinations, most of the bulk additive rather than wrong. Audited against a live account: Enquiries/Saved appeared **twice** (header figures *and* two large tiles, ~230px to say `1` and `0` twice); name and email appeared **three times** each; four detail cards spent ~470px printing back someone's own name and birthday, with two em-dashes where a college would go; **three separate rows said "Help"**, going to the Help Centre, the *public marketing* `/contact` page and the hostel chat; **three of four settings rows raised `coming soon`** — no notification-preferences screen and no password-change screen exists in the app; and four headings sat over one row each. Now: **detail rows carry no values, only gaps** — the right side reads `Add college & course` or nothing at all, and that silence is load-bearing, since "nothing on the right" can only mean "nothing to do" when a filled field is never mentioned (a working professional is never told to add a college). **One hero, chosen by `heroMode`** — a resident sees where they live, a seeker sees the portable-profile pitch, a seeker who finished it sees neither; someone in Room 105 does not need 200px arguing for an offer already taken. **8 sections → 3 plus the hero**, one rounded container per group with hairlines rather than a card per row, and the dead settings rows deleted rather than kept as furniture. New pure module `profileHub.ts` (16 tests, written against the real record so "All filled" is checked rather than hoped). **Two silent bugs found and fixed on the way**, both of which had shipped through a green build: the profile's **"Request to move out" opened nothing** — `<MoveOutSheet>` sat inside `Stat`, a component nothing renders, referencing three out-of-scope identifiers — and `ListingPage` called **`describeAvailability` without importing it** (a `ReferenceError` while rendering amenities) and read a non-existent `C.muted`. `vite build` uses esbuild and **does not typecheck**, and the suite is node-only with no rendering, so `tsc --noEmit` was the only thing that could catch any of them; it is not in the build. See [[Bugs]]. **Verified:** frontend suite 1377/1377 (87 files, +16), `tsc --noEmit` clean for every file touched, production build, architecture and branding checks passing. **Not verified:** not opened in a browser — no screenshot of the rebuilt hub at either audience. See [[Features]], [[Frontend]], [[Decisions#ADR-118|ADR-118]].

- **2026-08-26**: **Help splits in two: the hostel's complaints and Stayo's bugs, with answers before either.** Both channels already existed and neither was told apart in the product. `tenant_service_requests` (**9 live rows**) carries what the *hostel* fixes — repairs, cleaning, food, roommates; `platform_support_tickets` carries what *Stayo* fixes — and had **0 rows, ever**, despite a complete pipeline: a role-agnostic `POST /api/profile/support-tickets`, an admin queue at `/admin/reports`, a resolve endpoint writing an `admin_note` meant to be shown back, and an open-count badge in the admin shell. Two frontend reasons, no backend change needed for either. **The owner could not file at all** — More → Help's "Report a Bug" raised `stayoToast.info('Coming soon')`, and its FAQs were `mockFaqs` placeholder data. **And there was no guidance layer** — the page opened straight into a form, so someone who merely could not find a screen chose between filing a ticket and giving up. Now one **Help Centre** serves both audiences from one component (`helpCenter.ts` + `HelpCenter.tsx`), because owner and tenant have the same problem shape and building it twice is how two halves drift: search first, an 18-entry catalogue second, a form only third. **Every answer that resolves by going somewhere carries the button** rather than naming the tab — the same correction already applied to move-out. **`classifyProblem()` is the wall between the inboxes, applied while someone types**: a geyser described into the Stayo box offers the hostel channel instead, and vice versa on the tenant's hostel-contact page, which gained a row to the Stayo side. The nudge **never blocks** — the classifier is a keyword guess, so a wrong verdict costs a card, where a block would cost someone their only way to reach us; when both kinds of word appear ("the app won't let me report my geyser") **Stayo wins**, since the sentence is about the tool failing. Filing shows a **persistent acknowledgement**, not a toast that vanishes before it is read, and the report joins a list carrying its status and the admin's reply. Deep links are **pinned by a test** against a transcribed copy of the router's table, so a renamed route breaks the suite rather than shipping a 404 to someone already stuck. **Found and fixed on the way:** `/tenant/complaints` had shipped **two** buttons — "Raise a ticket" and "Report a bug" — and *both* wrote to `tenant_service_requests`. The bug form promised "Help us improve the app" and "our team will investigate", then filed a `MAINTENANCE` job with the **hostel owner**; `platform_support_tickets` was never touched. **5 of the 9 live rows** arrived that way, categorised "Payments", "Food ordering" and "Room services" — real app reports in a repair queue nobody could act on them from. That button and its config are deleted, the page now has one action, and one vocabulary runs through it: a resident raises **complaints** with their hostel and sends **reports** to Stayo. "Ticket", which meant both, is gone from every user-facing string (`My tickets` → `My complaints`, Room's `Tickets` → `Complaints`, `Ticket history` → `Complaint history`); internal identifiers keep it, since renaming those changes nothing anyone sees. The 5 existing rows were left alone — same-day test data with empty descriptions, and rewriting an owner's queue is worse than leaving it. See [[Bugs]]. **Verified:** frontend suite 1361/1361 (86 files, +24 new), production build, architecture check and branding check all passing. **Not verified:** no report has yet been filed end to end against a live admin queue — the table is still empty. `mockFaqs` is now unreferenced. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Decisions#ADR-117|ADR-117]].

- **2026-08-26**: **The tenant Room tab now shows facts, or nothing.** Two of its five sections were fiction: **Living status** read a table with **0 rows product-wide** that no owner screen could write, so it rendered "Available · Running normally" about hostels nobody had reported on; **Room facilities** were six hardcoded literals copied from the design mock, and every row wore the same Wi-Fi glyph. Both are gone. Facilities now come from the **APPROVED marketing revision** — `AmenitySchema` gained optional free-text `detail` and `schedule`, so an owner writes "Hot water · Attached bathroom · 6–10 AM · 6–10 PM" once and it appears on both the tenant's Room tab and Discovery, having passed the same admin review (no migration; `content` is jsonb). **Wi-Fi is deliberately not an amenity** — per room, never reviewed, never public, with a new editor on the owner's room sheet, which is why all 70 rooms were blank. **Reporting moved into the facility being reported**, replacing the six-tile Room services grid, so a ticket arrives pre-categorised. **A vacant bed became an invitation** through the existing share sheet. And **move-out finally has a tenant UI** — the endpoint has accepted `initiatedByRole: "TENANT"` all along — in the Room tab and on a new "Where you live" card on the profile, which also fixes the profile telling someone in their first hostel they have **"0 past stays"**. See [[Decisions#ADR-116|ADR-116]].

- **2026-08-25**: **"Where you'll be" — the Discover listing now shows a map, without a Google Maps key.** The listing had a "Getting around" block (Place ID directions, landmark, entrance photo) but no map, and rendering one needs *coordinates*, which the schema had nowhere, plus a keyed Maps API this project does not have. Both solved cheaply: `hostels.navigation` is `jsonb`, so optional **`lat`/`lng`** were added to `NavigationSchema` with **no migration**, and the map is an **OpenStreetMap `export/embed.html` iframe** — no API key, no SDK, no new dependency. An admin pastes `17.4542678, 78.6628497` straight from a Google search into one field; `parseCoordinates` accepts that shape (and parenthesised/degree-decorated variants), refuses anything ambiguous, refuses hemisphere letters rather than guessing sign conventions, and treats `0, 0` as "not entered" since it is both a real place in the Gulf of Guinea and what an empty numeric pair produces. The server rejects out-of-range values too — a swapped lat/lng puts a Hyderabad hostel in the Indian Ocean, and a confident pin in the wrong place is worse than no pin. Without coordinates the section degrades to the existing landmark/photo block rather than showing an empty frame, which is the state **3 of 4 production hostels** are in today. Section renamed from "Getting around" to **"Where you'll be"**, with the area line above the map.

- **2026-08-25**: **Fixed resuming a half-built hostel opening on the wrong step with empty fields.** `useHostelBuilder` picked its stage from `existingHostelId ? 'fill' : 'name'` — decided before any data loaded — and the hydration effect only corrected it inside `if (restored.length > 0)`, so a hostel with a name and **no floors** was stuck on "What's on ground floor?" with nothing to fill. The name was never restored at all (the resume read returns floors and rooms, not the hostel), and `isRestoring` tracked `existing.isLoading`, which goes false a render before hydration runs. Now a pure `resumeStage()` derives the step from what the hostel has, the name is read from the owner's hostel list, and the loading gate holds until hydration completes. Verified against live data: `Test1` was named with zero floors, exactly the reported state. See [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-25**: **A tenant now gets a WhatsApp reminder 24 hours before their activation link dies.** Invitations last seven days and said nothing in between, so the common failure was silent — someone meant to get to it, didn't, and found a dead link whose only remedy was asking the owner to resend. New template `stayo_tenant_invitation_expiry_reminder` (contract + payload builder pinned by tests, same shape as the approved invitation template: the URL button carries the **token alone**, since Meta stores the `/activate/` base), a selection service, and a **daily** cron at `/api/cron/invitation-expiry-reminders` with a **36-hour** look-ahead. Daily because Vercel's Hobby plan permits exactly one cron run per day and rejects anything more frequent **at deploy time** — an hourly schedule does not degrade, it fails the deployment. The wider window is what makes a daily cadence honest: with a 24h window on a 24h cadence someone could be reminded with minutes left, whereas at 36h every invitation is seen with 12h or more remaining, and the message states the real number rather than a fixed "24". `ACTIVATION_STARTED` invitations are **excluded** — `resolveByToken` stops applying the expiry check once activation begins, so a countdown there would be a deadline that does not exist. Already-expired links are skipped too: that is a notification of failure, not a reminder, and the fix belongs with the owner. De-duplication is keyed on the **invitation** in `system_event_logs` rather than a new column, so a resend's fresh seven days earns its own reminder and no migration is needed to make the marker work.

- **2026-08-25**: **The tenant can now see how long their activation link has left.** An invitation lasts seven days (`DEFAULT_INVITE_DAYS`) and nothing ever said so — people found out by clicking a dead link. The context now returns `link_expiry`, and a quiet banner under the progress card states it. Urgency is **earned, not constant**: six days out it reads "This link is valid until 31 Aug", it counts days inside 72 hours, switches to hours in the last day and minutes in the last hour (re-rendering every 30s only then), and on expiry names the way forward rather than dead-ending. Crucially, once activation has **started** it shows "Your place is held — finish whenever you're ready" and no clock at all, because `resolveByToken` skips the expiry check for `ACTIVATION_STARTED` — a countdown there would be a deadline that does not exist. Nothing is shown when there is no `expires_at` (a legacy profile-token link), rather than inventing one.

- **2026-08-25**: **Fixed "Activation link expired or already used" appearing on links that had not expired.** A regression from [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]], and one that only a real Discover tenant could expose. `computeState` derived `account_setup_completed` from `profile?.phone_verified && tenant.phone_1`, which was correct only because `profile` used to be **null** for an unbound tenancy. Resolving the invitee's existing account gave it a profile that already had `phone_verified: true` from the enquiry OTP, so account setup reported itself complete before running: the wizard skipped to ACTIVATE, `startActivation` never bound `tenants.profile_id`, and `activate()` — which finds the tenancy through the profile — threw a message about expiry that had nothing to do with expiry. The rule now tests `tenant.profile_id`, extracted to `activation-account-state.ts` with the regression pinned. No data repair needed. See [[Bugs]].
- **2026-08-25**: **Resident reviews get hostel-specific categories, a standalone Overall Experience rating, automatic topic/sentiment detection, and an admin insights view.** The review system [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]] shipped 2026-08-19 already had eligibility, moderation and a Discover-page write flow end to end (and, since 2026-08-24, an identity-aware public GET and an empty-state-free listing UI — [[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]], [[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]], both untouched by this change) — this extended it rather than rebuilding it, per [[Decisions#ADR-115|ADR-115]]. **Categories**: `rating_value`/`rating_location` (Airbnb's holiday-flat shape) dropped; `rating_maintenance`, `rating_room_comfort`, `rating_amenities`, `rating_wifi` added, alongside the carried-over cleanliness/food/safety/staff columns (`staff` now labelled "Staff & Management") — eight hostel-specific categories, migration `076_hostel_review_categories_v2.sql`. **Overall Experience is no longer derived** — the resident answers it directly as its own question in the write box, validated independently server-side, and Discover's headline average is the mean of these standalone answers rather than a mean-of-categories. **Automatic categorization + sentiment** (`review-categorization.ts`, a deterministic keyword-lexicon classifier — no model call, pure and unit-tested) reads a review's free-text comment and tags it with the categories it discusses plus a sentiment per category, written to a new `hostel_review_topics` table inside the same transaction as the review upsert. This is **structurally separate from moderation** — `reviewsService.moderate()` never reads it, and a negative sentiment never affects publish/reject. **A fourth review status, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`**, sits alongside `PENDING`/`PUBLISHED`/`REJECTED` with its own admin action and author-facing note; editing any review already returned it to `PENDING` regardless of status, so this needed no new state-machine logic. **New admin Insights tab** (`ReviewInsightsPanel.tsx`, `GET /api/platform-admin/reviews/insights`) — per-category mention counts, average rating and positive/neutral/negative split, filterable by category, sentiment, hostel and status — answering "what are residents talking about" as a genuinely separate question from the existing moderation queue's "should this be published". Discover-page additions: a "Resident Favourite" label (average ≥4.5 across ≥5 reviews), "Residents mention" highlight tags derived from categories rated ≥4 (no new table — computed at read time from data moderation already produced), category icons, and a "Verified Resident · Stayed N months" badge from a new snapshotted `stay_months` column (same not-a-live-join reasoning as the pre-existing `stayed_here`). Extracted the three near-duplicate hand-rolled 5-star loops (Discover's review section, the admin queue) into one shared `StarRating` component. Explicitly out of scope, matching the spec's own guidance: admin-configurable categories (hardcoded 8 for V1), rating shown on Discover search-result cards (the ask was the listing detail page), and server-side pagination for reviews (client-side reveal of the already-fetched batch). 2 new backend pure modules + 2 new/extended test files (`review-categorization.test.ts` new, `review-summary.test.ts` extended — 37 tests total for the reviews domain), 1 new backend service method (`insights`), 3 new/changed backend routes, 1 new shared frontend component, 1 new admin panel. **Verified:** Prisma schema validated against the real dev Supabase project (migration 076 applied there, alongside several older migrations that project turned out to be missing entirely — see [[Database]]); backend pure suite passing for the reviews domain (37/37) with no regression elsewhere; `typescript` installed as an actual devDependency in `apps/frontend` for the first time (a `typecheck` script existed with nothing in the dependency tree to run it) and `tsc --noEmit` runs clean of new errors in both apps; the full resident flow was driven end to end through a real browser (submit as a signed-in tenant across all applicable categories + Overall Experience + a mixed-sentiment comment → `PENDING` with correctly auto-detected topics → admin sees the detected topics and category breakdown, unaffected by [[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]]/[[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]]'s independent changes to the same page → Publish → appears on the public listing with highlight tags and the "too few to average" honesty rule at 1 review → Insights tab's stat cards and sentiment filter both correct); frontend architecture check and full frontend suite passing. **Not verified:** production build/deploy of this exact change in isolation; any browser other than headless Chromium; concurrent submissions/moderation. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Database]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Decisions#ADR-115|ADR-115]].

- **2026-08-25**: **Food scheduling becomes fully manual — the owner drags/taps dishes into a Timetable, no algorithm ever picks one.** Automatic schedule generation (vote-share/alphabetical), cross-day swap-dragging, and the nightly carry-forward clone were all removed ([[Decisions#ADR-114|ADR-114]]); the underlying schema needed no change since `food_menu_items` (the library) and `food_schedule_meals`/`food_schedule_meal_items` (2026-08-24, below) already modeled reusable items referenced by ordered per-cell lists. **Deleted:** `lib/services/food-schedule-generator.ts`, `lib/services/food/schedule-rebuild-policy.ts`, `POST /api/food/schedules/generate`, `lib/services/food/meal-swap.ts`, `POST /api/food/schedules/[id]/meals/swap`, and their tests; the frontend's `WeeklyScheduleGrid.tsx`, `DayRow.tsx`, `ScheduleMealPickerSheet.tsx`, `dragSwap.ts`, `pickerSelection.ts`. **New:** `POST /api/food/schedules` — an idempotent "create an empty DRAFT schedule if this hostel+month doesn't have one yet" call (`200` if it already exists, `201` if just created), replacing the generator as the only creator of a schedule row; no dish is ever chosen or copied by it. **New owner surface:** `TimetablePage.tsx` (`/owner/food/timetable`) — Monday–Sunday day tabs, one active meal section at a time, a Food-Library panel scoped to that section with search, drag-or-tap to add (handle-only drag so a scroll never accidentally adds an item), drag-with-handle to reorder, a plain × to remove — replacing the old 7-day grid and tap-checklist picker sheet. Month navigation (Previous/current/Next) creates that month's row on demand and **never** copies another month's content. `PATCH .../meals/[mealId]` — the one remaining write primitive for add/remove/reorder alike — gained an `expectedUpdatedAt` optimistic-concurrency guard (reusing the deleted swap endpoint's own `updated_at`-equality technique), answering `409 STALE_WRITE` rather than silently overwriting a newer edit from another tab. **Product rules newly made explicit and enforced:** no duplicate dish within one meal slot (same dish is fine on a different day/slot); no cap on dishes per meal; **an incomplete week cannot be published** — a deliberate, scoped reversal of `publishChecks.ts`'s prior "checks never block" stance, only for emptiness (`variety`/`runs` remain informational), gating the Publish button off a short summary line rather than an enumerated list; every edit persists immediately with optimistic UI + rollback on failure, no Save button anywhere; renaming a Food Library item updates its Timetable display without duplicating it (soft-delete already prevented a broken reference, unchanged); editing an already-published schedule keeps its existing live-immediately/undo-toast behavior exactly as before. The nightly cron lost its schedule-cloning responsibility and was renamed `food-carry-forward` → `food-expiry`, keeping only its unrelated voting-period/poll auto-close jobs. New frontend pure module `timetableDnd.ts` (26 tests); `publishChecks.ts` extended with `incompleteCells`/`canPublish` (test count 16→20); `weekGrid.ts`'s `WeekGridCell` gained `updated_at`. **Verified:** backend pure suite 727/729 (56 files, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures as before); frontend suite 997/997 (66 files) — both re-run clean after every edit in this change, not just once at the end; frontend production build (`vite build`) and architecture check both passing, `TimetablePage` code-splits into its own chunk with no compile errors; backend `tsc --noEmit` shows zero new errors from this change (the food routes' pre-existing implicit-`any` `tx` callback warnings are unchanged, matching a convention already present in every other food route's `$transaction` call, not something introduced here — confirmed by grep across untouched files); `prisma validate` passing (schema itself is unmodified by this change). **Not verified:** no test database in this environment, so the new/changed routes are verified by code review, `tsc`, and the pure-logic suites above, not a live request against real Postgres; not exercised in a running browser — this environment has no way to run the Next.js dev server (missing `.env`/DB credentials) or a headless browser against the Vite dev server. See [[Food]] §18, [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Decisions#ADR-114|ADR-114]].

- **2026-08-24**: **A scheduled meal can now hold more than one dish.** `food_schedule_meals` modeled "Monday Lunch" as exactly one `menu_item_id`/`item_name` — picking a second dish silently overwrote the first, so a real combination like Rice/Dal/Curry/Chutney was unrepresentable. A new child table, `food_schedule_meal_items` (ordered by `display_order`, cascade-deleted with its parent cell), now holds a cell's real content; the weekly grain — exactly one row per `(schedule, day_of_week, meal_type)` — is untouched. `food_schedule_meals.menu_item_id`/`.item_name` were **kept, not dropped** — they become an auto-maintained denormalized cache (first item's id, names comma-joined) written on every mutation, purely so `src/services/marketing/mess-import.ts` (the Discovery-listing mess-menu importer, which reads `item_name` directly and was out of scope here) keeps working unmodified ([[Decisions#ADR-113|ADR-113]]). `PATCH /api/food/schedules/[id]/meals/[mealId]`'s body changed from `{menuItemId}` to `{menuItemIds: string[]}` — full-replace, not overwrite — validated by a new pure module (`lib/services/food/meal-items.ts`) against the hostel's own active library, rejecting any cross-hostel or wrong-meal-type id. The swap endpoint's concurrency guard moved from a `menu_item_id` equality check to `updated_at` equality, since only the latter stays a complete state fingerprint once a cell can hold N items. The owner picker sheet was rewritten as a checklist — check/uncheck dishes, Save commits the whole ordered selection at once, Cancel discards it — replacing the old immediate-overwrite-and-close flow; "Move to another day" is now disabled while a checklist edit is unsaved, since firing that swap used to force-close the sheet and would otherwise silently drop pending changes. Every owner and tenant surface that displayed a dish name (Today card, weekly grid, Kitchen sheet + its WhatsApp message, Month History, owner Home line, both tenant Food/Home tabs) now renders the full list via one shared formatter (`formatCellItems`, "Rice • Dal • Curry • Chutney"); the weekly grid's chip shows a compact "first dish + N more" preview rather than growing to fit every item. The publish pre-flight checklist's "one dish dominates a meal type" and "repeats on consecutive days" warnings now compare cells by an **order-independent set of dishes** rather than the joined display string, so reordering a cell's items can't silently make an otherwise-identical repeat invisible to those checks. The dormant monthly-voting system (`food_votes`, already multi-select for a different purpose) and Food Polls were both left untouched, as was the Discovery-listing's own mess-menu copy. 1 new backend pure module + 10 tests, 1 new frontend pure module + 9 tests, plus new/updated cases in `weekGrid.test.ts` and `publishChecks.test.ts` covering add/remove/reorder/empty-meal/migrated-single-item-data/cross-hostel-isolation. **Verified:** Prisma schema validation passing; backend pure suite 741/743 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures); frontend suite 984/984; frontend production build + architecture + branding checks passing. **Not verified:** no test database in this environment, so the write paths are verified by code review and pure-logic tests rather than a live request; the migration (additive only — new table + a data backfill, no column drops) was written but not applied to any database, since `schema.prisma` already carried unrelated pre-existing uncommitted work (hostel lat/long coordinates) that `db push` would have applied together with it — left for a deliberate, separate decision. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Database]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Food]] §17, [[Decisions#ADR-113|ADR-113]].

- **2026-08-25**: **The agreement stops being fine print and becomes a word given.** The tenant Agreement screen showed rules and two signature pads and **never stated how long the stay was for** — someone could sign an eleven-month commitment without the number appearing anywhere, and the owner got a signature carrying no evidence the length had been understood. The data was there all along (`agreement_duration_months`, start/end dates, contract rent and deposit, populated on 8/8 production rows); it was just never sent to the client. Now: a **"Your word" card** before signing, showing the term as a headline and **two columns** — what the tenant commits to and what the hostel commits to (the room held, rent that does not move, the deposit returned) — because listing only one side reads as extraction; a **commitment sheet** on Confirm restating it in the first person with two unticked checkboxes and an **"I give my word"** button; and a **handshake** afterwards naming both parties and the term. The acknowledgement is **recorded** on `Agreement.content_snapshot` with the time, IP, user agent and the exact sentence shown — an acknowledgement the system does not keep gives the owner nothing to trust — and needs **no migration**. Crucially it **never threatens**: `notice_period_days` is NULL on every live template, so move-out records no violation and a lock-in claim would simply be false; the sheet says so plainly — *"this is a promise, not a trap"* — and a test forbids penalty language. A hostel with no duration on file skips the ceremony and signs as before. See [[Decisions#ADR-112|ADR-112]].

- **2026-08-25**: **The Identity screen stops asking what we already know, gets a real date picker, and stays readable after dark.** Three fixes from one round of screenshots. **Gender** is no longer asked when the hostel's own type answers it — `BOYS → Male`, `GIRLS → Female`, field hidden; `CO_LIVING`, `WORKING_PROS` and an *unset* type still ask, because `hostels.hostel_type` is nullable and was **NULL on half the production hostels**, and guessing there would write a gender nobody supplied into a permanent record. A gender already on the record always beats a derived one. **Date of birth** was an `<input type="date">` with its calendar affordance hidden by `[&::-webkit-calendar-picker-indicator]:opacity-0`, so it looked dead and ordered month-first; it is now a three-column day/month/year sheet — a birth date is a bad fit for a calendar, where today's month is never the answer and paging back is ~240 taps — opening near a typical resident's year, clamping 31 Jan → 28/29 Feb, showing live age, and parsing `YYYY-MM-DD` without `new Date()` so the day cannot shift a timezone west. **Text contrast** now follows the sky: the wizard body renders directly over the time-of-day gradient with no surface of its own, but used fixed daytime greys, so labels faded out at dusk and night — `skyEnv()` gained `onSkyTitle`/`onSkyBody`/`onSkyLabel`/`onSkyShadow`, shared through a new `SkyProvider`. See [[Decisions#ADR-111|ADR-111]].

- **2026-08-25**: **An invited tenant is now met as themselves, not as a stranger.** Onboarding asked a Stayo Discover invitee to OTP the mobile we had just WhatsApp'd their invitation link to, then asked for a "Gmail ID" and rejected the one they gave — their own — as already registered. One dropped link caused all of it: `convertToInvitation` never carried `seeker_profile_id` onto the tenancy, so `resolveByToken` reported no account for someone who plainly had one, and every check downstream was correct on wrong inputs. Now the account is resolved (bound → the enquiry's `seeker_profile_id` via the already-indexed `converted_tenant_id` → a guarded contact match), **a number we can already vouch for is not re-verified**, and **the email is not collected at all** — it comes from the account, else the invitation, else a phone-derived placeholder, and the `@gmail.com`-only rule went with the field. Editing the number still costs an OTP, which is the one thing that re-arms verification. Trust has two independent sources: the linked account verified that exact number at enquiry time, or the link was delivered to it over WhatsApp — recorded in a new `tenant_invitations.whatsapp_delivered_at` (migration `20260825090000`) that stores **only success**, so the rare WhatsApp-failed-then-emailed path falls into "ask for the OTP" without a branch of its own. The column is deliberately **not** declared in `schema.prisma` — ~52 unselected reads of that table would 500 until the migration landed, which is the 2026-08-22 outage — and is read via raw SQL that degrades to "unknown". Caught while building: resolving the profile stopped `saveAccount` from delegating to `startActivation`, the only path that *binds* `tenants.profile_id` and therefore the only thing that runs the one-live-tenancy check; the condition is now `!tenant.profile_id`. See [[Decisions#ADR-110|ADR-110]] and [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **An enquiry can no longer reach an owner without a phone number — and the test file that should have caught it had not run in months.** `POST /api/discover/enquiries` validated the hostel slug and the stay details and nothing about the sender; `createEnquiry` wrote `student_phone: seeker.phone` whatever that was, including `null`. The `phone_verified` gate lived entirely in `EnquiryPage`, so anything not going through that screen could put an **uncallable lead** in an owner's inbox — and being able to call back is the owner's whole reason for receiving one. Reachable since [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]] made `phone: null` the normal shape of a new account. The guard now sits in the **service**, before any database work, so a future second caller inherits it; and it requires a **number on file rather than `phone_verified` true**, because [[Decisions#ADR-034|ADR-034]] deliberately lets signup through with a `SKIPPED` row when WhatsApp cannot deliver, and gating on the flag would refuse those seekers outright. Writing the test exposed a quieter problem: **`tests/discovery-service.test.ts` was reporting zero tests, not passing ones.** It is in the `test:pure` allowlist, but `discovery-service.ts` imports `whatsapp-template-delivery`, which builds a `MetaWhatsAppProvider` at module scope and throws without `WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN` — so the suite died at import, which a summary that counts failures cannot distinguish from success. Mocking that boundary revived it and surfaced a second staleness (`hostel_marketing_revisions.findMany`, added to the service by `fillCoverPhotos`, missing from the prisma mock) that was breaking 10 more. The file went from **0 to 23 running tests**. See [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-25**: **"+ Add hostel" was resuming an old hostel instead of adding one.** Reported as the wizard skipping its first two steps — tapping the button landed straight on the Rooms screen, already showing "Ground floor" and "First floor", never asking for a name. It was not skipping anything: it was not creating a hostel at all. `onAddHostel` navigated to `/owner/hostels/:id/build` whenever `hostelInProgress` existed, and that is **any hostel in the account with zero rooms**; resuming deliberately starts at the Rooms stage because Name and Floors are already done *for that old hostel*. The floor chips on screen were the abandoned hostel's real floors, read back from the server. Not an edge case — while any zero-room hostel existed, a new hostel could **never** be created from Home, and zero-room hostels are precisely what the incremental-write builder leaves behind, so one bad build disabled the button permanently. Fixed to always go to `/owner/hostels/new`; resuming keeps its honest home on the getting-started card ("finish setting up your hostel"), and a half-built hostel is editable from its own Rooms tab regardless. Found alongside: `hostelInProgress` is a **dead prop** on `OwnerHomeDashboard` — declared, destructured, never rendered — so the "continue building" card that would have made resume reachable does not exist, which is why the hijacked button was the only route to it; left in place pending a decision. The real cure is the [draft-then-create spec](../superpowers/specs/2026-08-24-add-hostel-draft-then-create-design.md), where no half-built hostel exists on the server for the button to be confused by. 1084 frontend tests, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **The floor form got a real structure, and its numbering picker turned out not to work.** An audit of the Add Hostel Rooms step, prompted by a request for proper sectioning, turned up a functional bug first: **`onPatternChange` was a bare `setPattern`**, and the pattern only ever reached `resizeFloorRooms`, which numbers rooms *as it creates them*. So changing the scheme after the rooms existed highlighted a chip and renumbered nothing — set the count with `101, 102…` selected, tap `G-01, G-02…`, and the list below keeps `101, 102, 103`. Worse, the pattern is **one value for the whole builder** while rooms are numbered per floor at generation time, so changing it on the second floor left the first on the old scheme: one building numbered two ways, saved that way, no warning. Fixed by `renumberBuilding` across every floor, renumbering **only** rooms whose current number is exactly what the old scheme would have produced for their position — a room the owner renamed keeps its name, and the check is on the number rather than the `customised` flag because that flag is set by editing rent or sharing too, and changing a price is not a claim on a name. On the layout: **five separate things told the owner which floor they were on** — progress bar, journey label, floor strip, eyebrow, footer summary — stacking until the first control sat **42% down a phone screen**; the eyebrow is gone and the journey label no longer repeats the floor name, since the strip highlights it and the heading says it. The screen is now **three cards, one per question** in the order an owner asks it — how many, what they're like, what they're called — where previously only the middle block was carded and the count, numbering and list were bare, so nothing indicated which controls went together. Things moved to where they are about: the tally sits beside the counter instead of at the foot of the list where it read as a fourth room row; numbering sits with the rooms it names, labelled "Numbered across the whole building" so its scope is stated rather than implied; and "Copy these 3 rooms to the next floor" — the biggest time-saver on the screen — moved out of an unsectioned float above the footer into the card holding the rooms it copies. Teaching copy now appears only on an empty floor ([[Decisions#ADR-109|ADR-109]]). 10 new tests, all pure; 1084 frontend tests, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **Add Hostel's floors are switchable, and finishing now sweeps up what the walk missed.** The builder walked floors strictly forward — Back one, "Save floor & continue" one — so a floor's position in the queue was its only identity, and correcting a room on the ground floor from the second meant reversing through everything between. There is now a strip of floor chips at the top of the Rooms step; tapping one switches instantly, since every floor's rooms already live in local state and nothing is written on a switch. **The substance of the change is what free navigation takes away.** You previously *could not* reach floor 3 without floor 1 having been written on the way past; now you can — fill the ground floor, tap across to the second, press finish — so **finishing writes every floor still holding unsaved rooms**. Without that the switcher would silently lose work. The sweep is **validated in full before it writes anything** (`sweepBlocker` names the offending floor and switches to it), and runs **sequentially rather than in parallel** so a failure part-way leaves the earlier floors written rather than racing. It is only cheap at all because saving a floor became idempotent in [[Decisions#ADR-097|ADR-097]] — re-saving an already-written floor is a no-op rather than the `Room 101 already exists` conflict it used to be. Each chip carries its own state — ✓ written, ● entered but not written yet, plain = still needs rooms — so the strip is the progress map as well as the navigation; free movement is only safe if the owner can see what is outstanding. The primary button moved with it: it used to go to `activeIndex + 1`, which with a switcher marches past floors already filled and stops short of ones that were skipped, so it now continues to the next floor still *needing rooms*, wrapping to the start, and says "finish" only when none do ([[Decisions#ADR-108|ADR-108]]). 22 new tests, all pure; 1075 frontend tests, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]].

- **2026-08-24**: **Home finally shows money going out, and is careful about what it calls the result.** The owner home page was entirely money **in** — "August Collection, ₹16,000 of ₹57,000" — which answers *how much have I collected* and never the question an owner opens the app with: *am I ahead this month*. ₹16,000 collected means one thing against ₹4,200 of spending and quite another against ₹30,000. The Collection card is now the **month** card: Collected / Spent / **Left**, with the bar showing what has already gone out eaten from what came in. **"Left", never profit** — it is cash received minus cash spent, blind to unpaid dues, deposits held and anything accrued, and this codebase does not dress a partial number as a whole one. A month that cost more than it brought in says **"Overspent by ₹X"** rather than rendering "Left −₹2,300", which reads as a rendering bug; overspending is an ordinary hostel month (a deposit refunded, a roof repaired), not an error state. Plus **one Action Center line in a month where a category spiked**, and nothing there otherwise. That rule is deliberately much stricter than the Money screen's existing per-category `trend >= 35`, which has **no absolute floor** — ₹100 → ₹200 is +100% and fires. Among nineteen rows that is tolerable noise; on Home it is the only thing said about money going out, so a false alarm costs trust in the whole surface. `detectSpendAnomaly` adds a ₹1,000 floor, requires **both** floors rather than either, ranks by **rupees risen rather than percentage** (a ₹22,000 rise on groceries beats a 300% rise on stationery), refuses to speak without a prior-month baseline, and names one category. The Money screen's own rule is untouched. **Composed in the route, not added to `portfolioService`** — that service documents its own invariant (every metric from `hostel_daily_snapshots`, no raw transactional table queried inside it), and the automated check names only payments, obligations and tenants, so putting the expense query there **would have passed the script while breaking the rule the script exists to protect**; `getMonthSpendSummary()` is 4 queries against `getAllExpenses`'s ~13. The spend half is `.catch(() => null)` and the card falls back to collection-only rather than showing "Spent ₹0" — an absence, not a claim ([[Decisions#ADR-107|ADR-107]]). 29 new tests (15 backend, 14 frontend), all pure; 1053 frontend tests, backend pure 800 passing at its documented 2-failure baseline, production build, `check:architecture` and all 8 backend invariants clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[APIs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **The Leads inbox is grouped by what is still owed on each enquiry, not by when it arrived.** Reported with a screenshot: four "Accepted" cards filling the screen, each offering Call and WhatsApp as though there were still a conversation to have, and a new enquiry would have landed *among* them rather than above them. Now five groups ordered by urgency — **Needs you** and **Accepted — invitation not sent** open by default, **On hold**, **Invited & joined** and **Closed** collapsed to a header line with a count, empty groups not rendered at all. The accepted group is deliberately **named after the missing step** rather than the status, and its hint says outright "They are not tenants until the invitation goes out" — a test asserts that wording, because shortening it back to "Accepted" is precisely what created [[Decisions#ADR-104|ADR-104]]'s confusion. **Each card's dark button is now that lead's actual next step** — *Accept & invite*, *Send invitation*, *Review*, or **nothing at all** for a lead with no outstanding work; WhatsApp previously held that position on every card, so the most prominent thing on a finished enquiry was a conversation there was no reason to start. **"Needs you" is ordered by `lead_score`** so the person most likely to take a bed is first, while settled groups are plain recency — they are being looked up, not ranked — with a final tiebreak on id so the order cannot wobble between renders. An **unrecognised status surfaces in "Needs you"** rather than vanishing into a collapsed group, and an **active search opens collapsed groups** so a match is never hidden behind a chevron ([[Decisions#ADR-105|ADR-105]]). **And the page limit that could hide a lead entirely is fixed** ([[Decisions#ADR-106|ADR-106]]). The inbox fetched one page of `limit: 20`, and `listLeads` orders by **`lead_score desc`** — not by whether anything is owed — so a brand-new enquiry, which scores low, sorted *below* settled high-score leads and could fall off the end of the page unseen. Collapsing the settled groups hid that from view without fixing it. The inbox now fetches **two pages, not one** — actionable statuses and settled ones — through a new `statuses=` multi-value filter on `GET /api/leads`, so the actionable half is bounded by outstanding work rather than by accumulated history. The filter validates against `LEAD_STATUSES`, drops unrecognised values, and **ignores a filter naming nothing valid rather than silently matching every lead**. Settled leads page on "Show older" — `useInfiniteQuery` with `page`, not a growing `limit`, because `limit` is clamped to 50 server-side and a larger request would be silently cut back with the button appearing to do nothing. The status sets are exported from the same module that does the grouping, with a test asserting the two partition all eleven statuses identically, so fetch and display cannot drift. If the actionable half ever overflows one page the list says so and points at search — that is the half where an unseen lead costs money, so it must not fail quietly. **Not fixed:** the server-side ordering itself, which would need a status-priority `CASE` in raw SQL or a stored column. 32 new tests (26 frontend, 6 backend); 1039 frontend tests, backend pure 785 passing at its documented 2-failure baseline, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[APIs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **A lead is marked accepted only when the invitation actually goes out.** Reported with a screenshot of four enquiries all reading "Accepted" that nobody had been invited to. Accept was two writes pretending to be one act: the button PATCHed the lead to `ACCEPTED` and *then* opened the pre-filled Add Tenant wizard — it had to, because `convertToInvitation` hard-required `status === "ACCEPTED"`. Close the wizard instead of submitting and the first write stood alone, and nothing in the product distinguishes "accepted, invitation pending" from "accepted and invited", so the Leads tab read as four completed handovers that never happened. **Accept now writes nothing** — relabelled **"Accept & invite"**, it opens the wizard, and the lead advances straight to `INVITED` inside the conversion endpoint when the invitation really goes out; abandoning the wizard leaves the enquiry exactly as it was. The backend precondition moved with it: `status === "ACCEPTED"` became the pure `canConvertLeadToInvitation(status, converted_tenant_id)`, asking "is this still open to conversion" rather than "has it been accepted" — every open status, `ON_HOLD` and `ACCEPTED` allowed, `REJECTED`/`LOST`/`INVITED`/`JOINED`/already-converted refused in their own words. `ACCEPTED` stays allowed so leads parked there by the old flow (the four in the screenshot) are still convertible; `ON_HOLD` is newly allowed, because an invitation is a stronger commitment than an acceptance and demanding an un-hold first would only dead-end an on-hold lead whose owner pressed Accept. Nothing is lost by not writing `ACCEPTED`: it sends no notification — only `REJECTED` does — so the removed PATCH cost the tenant nothing. A test asserting the old rule was **deleted rather than worked around**, and now asserts the opposite with a comment saying why ([[Decisions#ADR-104|ADR-104]]). 9 new backend tests; 1013 frontend tests, backend pure 779 passing at its documented 2-failure baseline, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **Alerts has a search box, and it looks in all four lists rather than the one you have open.** The owner Alerts tab holds Leads, Messages, Renewals and Requests behind four chips and had no search at all. The obvious build — filter the open chip — is the wrong tool: an owner looking for one person does not know which list they are filed under, so typing "Riya" on Leads and getting nothing means concluding she is not in Stayo when she is in Renewals one chip away. Instead one box above the chips searches everything: the open chip shows its matches, **the chip counts follow the query** so the tabs are the result summary, and any other category with hits appears as an "Also found in — Renewals (1)" chip that switches to it. A lead is findable by name, phone, hostel, source or status — by the **label an owner reads**, "Ready to join", never `READY_TO_JOIN`. **Phone matching is numeric on both sides**, reduced to the national tail, so `8046`, `8008046952` and `918008046952` all find `+918008046952`; digits match phone fields only, never prose, and never below three digits. That last rule exists because the tests caught two real defects in the first version: with the number in the text haystack, `80` matched it as a plain substring (and `80` matches roughly every Indian mobile in the account), and the full country-code form failed to match at all. Deliberately local rather than a new endpoint — the lists are already in memory, and `/api/owner/search` ([[Decisions#ADR-044|ADR-044]]) stays the account-wide tool, which notably does not cover leads ([[Decisions#ADR-103|ADR-103]]). 23 new tests, all pure; 1013 frontend tests, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]].

- **2026-08-24**: **A signed-in resident was told to sign in to write a review — and the empty reviews section is gone entirely.** Reported with a screenshot showing the account signed in on Profile and "Sign in to write a review" on the listing. The backend was right at every step; it was never told who was asking. `PUBLIC_ROUTES` is prefix-matched and the public branch of `middleware.ts` **strips every identity header**, so `GET /api/discover/hostels/:slug/reviews` — public by that prefix — could never see a session, and eligibility always came back `SIGNED_OUT`. The same trap had already been hit and fixed for the *write* (`requiresSessionDespitePublicPrefix`, whose own doc comment describes exactly this symptom for `POST`), but the read needed the opposite exception — accept a session if offered — which no category expressed. Added a third: **`allowsOptionalIdentity`**, running the *same* verification as any authenticated route (Supabase JWT, legacy fallback, revocation deny-list, idle timeout) and passing identity through on success. The important half is that every 401 on those paths becomes **"continue anonymously"** instead: a stale token must not turn a public listing into an error, and a revoked one must not be honoured — so the checks are not relaxed, only their failure mode is. Scoped to one path and one verb, with tests asserting it does not leak to `/api/discover/enquiries`, `/api/discover/saved`, the listing route, or any write ([[Decisions#ADR-101|ADR-101]]). Separately, and reversing a copy decision recorded with [[Decisions#ADR-086|ADR-086]]: **an empty reviews section now renders nothing at all** — no heading, no "No reviews yet", no explanation of who may review, no Sign in card, and no locked write box for someone who has never lived there. The old reasoning was "a blank space reads as neglect; this reads as a rule", but every new listing carried it, so the explanation was the section's normal state rather than an exception, and it pushed the price and Enquire button down the page. It appears when there are reviews to read or when the reader is a resident who can add one, and stays collapsed while loading rather than flashing a heading that then disappears ([[Decisions#ADR-102|ADR-102]]). The rule itself is unchanged — it simply is not narrated to people it does not apply to. 4 new backend tests; 990 frontend tests, backend pure 771 passing at its documented 2-failure baseline, production build and `check:architecture` clean. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **Add Hostel audited screen by screen; floors and rooms can now be deleted.** Reported as "the fields to enter are not appearing" — they were rendering. `stepStyles.textInput` drew every input as a transparent box with one `--border` underline, and that token is **1.12:1** against the page, **fainter than the decorative graph-paper grid behind it** (1.21:1); WCAG asks 3:1 of a control boundary. New `--field-border` token at ≥3:1 on both themes, filled surfaces, focus rings, and `placeholder:font-normal` so "Sunrise Residency" stops reading as an answer already given ([[Decisions#ADR-098|ADR-098]]). Auditing the rest turned up **two hard blockers**. The Review screen offers an edit pencil on every floor, and taking it led back to a Save button that re-posted existing rooms and got `Room 101 already exists` — **every backward move past a saved floor was a dead end**. Saving a floor is now idempotent: the body is the floor as it should be, creating what is new, updating what changed, retiring what was dropped and **reviving** a retired number rather than inserting twice (the unique index covers inactive rows, so the old `is_active: true` clash check would have failed on the index with a raw Postgres error — a latent bug closed by the same change). It refuses, by name, to remove a room a tenant lives in or shrink a room below the people in it. The decision is the pure `planFloorRoomSave`, **20 tests with no database** — this repo has none provisioned, so rules living only inside a Prisma transaction are untestable ([[Decisions#ADR-097|ADR-097]]). Second blocker: **Back from Floors → "Create hostel" created a second, orphaned hostel**, and "Raise the floors" created a duplicate set of floors on every press; both are now idempotent. Also: one `<form>` around every step so **Enter works at all** (it did nothing anywhere); a progress bar that advances floor by floor and names the floor you are on, rather than "Step 3 of 4" for five screens; a disabled button that states its reason (the message written for it sat inside a click handler a disabled button never reaches); floor renaming after creation (`renameFloor` was wired up and passed to nothing); single-room deletion; rent fields that accept "6,000" and ignore the scroll wheel; no more orange warning on arriving at an empty floor; and the Floors step stopped saying "Watch it go up · only 5 fit in the picture" about an illustration **deleted from that page nine months earlier**. Separately, **floors and rooms can now be deleted** — both routes existed with no frontend caller anywhere, so an owner could add either and never remove it. (**A first version of this entry claimed hostels had the same gap. They did not** — archiving a hostel has long been wired to the dashboard's hostel card menu via `ArchiveHostelModal`; a duplicate removal card briefly built on the hostel Overview screen was deleted, and that screen now opens the existing modal instead.) A floor and a room are **really deleted**, unlike a hostel archive, and the room confirmation says so in as many words. Every refusal names the fix rather than the failure: an occupied room says move them out, a *reserved* one says cancel the invite (different problem, different fix), a floor with rooms says delete the rooms first. A floor is deletable only when empty, which is the point — it must never become a way to delete rooms nobody looked at — and that exposed a second bug: the Rooms tab rendered `null` for a floor with no rooms, so a deletable floor was **invisible and therefore undeletable**. Eligibility is one pure `propertyRemoval.ts`; when the server refuses anyway, its own message is shown instead of a generic apology ([[Decisions#ADR-099|ADR-099]]). Finally, **an archived hostel with no history can be deleted for good** — reported by an owner staring at two junk hostels, "ABC Hostel" and "test", whose Archived tab offered **Reactivate and nothing else**. New `DELETE /api/hostels/:id/permanent`, a separate path so the archive route's contract is untouched and it cannot be hit by accident. Two server-enforced conditions: already archived (deletion is a two-step, nothing live dies in one press), and **zero** tenants, payments, obligations, allocations, agreements, receipts, expenses and enquiries. The refusal names what it found and says the hostel *stays archived and its history stays intact*, so it never reads as data loss. Removes rooms → floors → hostel in one transaction (neither carries `onDelete: Cascade`); `hostels` has 40+ child relations, so an FK nobody counted surfaces as "still has records attached", never a 500. The one irreversible action in the app, so it asks for the name to be typed ([[Decisions#ADR-100|ADR-100]]). **Two corrections to what I claimed earlier in this same entry:** that archiving had no frontend caller, and that archived hostels were invisible to owners — **both wrong**, both from grepping for names the code does not use, and both contradicted by the vault's own `Features.md`, which already documented the wiring. 61 new tests (36 backend pure, 25 frontend); 990 frontend tests, backend pure 746 passing at its documented 2-failure baseline, `check:architecture` + `check:invariants` + production build clean, `tsc` at baseline both apps. **Known and not fixed:** `muted-foreground` (3.54:1) and `warning` (3.28:1) are still under AA for body text — that is the shared product palette and repaints every screen. **Not verified in a browser.** See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]], [[Bugs]].

- **2026-08-24**: **You can sign up with an email and a password again — and the phone still waits for the enquiry.** Since 2026-08-16 the Sign Up tab was a single "Continue with Google" button, which quietly made a Google account a **prerequisite for using Stayo at all**: `AuthContext.signUpTenant()` and `POST /api/auth/tenant-signup` still existed, simply with no way to reach them. The tab now takes **name, email, password, confirm password**, with Google kept below an OR divider — two ways in, neither privileged ([[Decisions#ADR-096|ADR-096]]). **What deliberately did not come back is the phone/OTP step**: [[Decisions#ADR-078|ADR-078]] moved phone verification to the moment it is needed — sending an enquiry — and it stays there, so `phone` became **optional** across `TenantSignupSchema`, `selfSignUpTenant()` and `signUpTenant()`, and a password account is born the **same shape a Google one already is** (`phone: null`, `phone_verified: false`), getting its verified number from `EnquiryPage`'s inline confirm → OTP → send flow. A phone sent to the endpoint anyway is **still** OTP-gated by the unchanged `resolveSignupPhoneVerification()` — the rule permits *no* number, never an *unverified* one. The duplicate-phone lookup now runs **only when a phone was supplied**, because `profiles.phone` is nullable *and* unique and querying it with `null` would match the first phone-less account and reject every signup after it. Validation lives in a pure `@shared/lib/tenantSignupForm` (name ≥ 2, email shape, password 8–64, confirm must match), mirroring the backend schema rather than replacing it, so the modal stays a renderer under this repo's jsdom-less test harness; errors are per-field and clear as you fix them. 14 new tests (12 frontend, 2 backend), 965 frontend tests and 13 in `tenant-self-signup` all passing, `check:architecture` + production build clean, `tsc` at baseline in both apps. **Found while tracing the flow, logged not fixed:** `POST /api/discover/enquiries` never re-checks `phone_verified` server-side — `EnquiryPage` is the only gate, so a direct call can put a phone-less lead in an owner's inbox. Predates this change; see [[Bugs]]. **Not verified in a browser** — no signup was actually performed against a running stack. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]].

- **2026-08-23**: **Money export now asks who the file is for, not what format you want.** The existing `ExportExpensesModal` was scope-first with the format hardcoded — a data-dump model. But **every export an owner makes is handed to somebody else**: an accountant, a bank officer, a partner, a manager; only bank reconciliation is for himself, and he has no opinion about CSV versus XLSX. The sheet now offers four named documents — *For my accountant* (Excel, four sheets), *Proof of income* (PDF), *Bank reconciliation* (Excel, payouts with references and tenants indented underneath), *Who owes me* (printable PDF chase list) — and format, columns and period defaults follow from that choice ([[Decisions#ADR-093|ADR-093]]). This **dissolves the collections-versus-payouts split** (the owner never picks a data domain) and pulled **expenses** in, since a CA cannot file from income alone; `ExportExpensesModal` is deleted rather than left as a second inconsistent export on one tab. Its "Current view" scope is the one capability dropped. **Proof of income keeps verified and self-reported money in two separate sections with their own subtotals, always both present** ([[Decisions#ADR-094|ADR-094]]) — most of an owner's rent is cash he typed in himself, a credit officer skims footnotes, and blending would present self-reported income as third-party-verified. The rule is a pure `proofOfIncomeSections()` rather than a string inside a PDF drawing call, because a rule that only exists inside a renderer cannot be tested. **Periods are sent by name and resolved server-side** ([[Decisions#ADR-095|ADR-095]]) — India's FY is April–March and a Jan–Dec export is not slightly wrong for an accountant, it is useless; the January case (where the current FY began in the *previous* calendar year) has its own test, and a reversed custom range is refused rather than silently repaired. **Rendering was split from gathering** into an I/O-free `export-documents.ts` so the genuinely risky half is testable without a database: pdf-lib's standard fonts are WinAnsi, so `₹` throws outright and so does any Telugu or Devanagari tenant name — money is written `Rs.` and names are sanitised, both with tests. Exports **compose the same read models the screens use**, with rent-received sharing the month block's exact definitions, so an exported total cannot disagree with an on-screen one. Every document carries provenance (period, hostels, row count, total, generated-at, "Figures as recorded in Stayo"), and a preflight line says what is in the file before it is built. Share uses the Web Share API — a `wa.me` link cannot carry a file — and the button is hidden rather than promising WhatsApp it cannot deliver. 2 new endpoints, 39 new tests (25 backend pure, 14 frontend); `tsc` at its 539 baseline both apps; 953 frontend tests and all checks at baseline. **Verified live:** all four documents generate against the real database, the FY resolved to Apr 2026 – Mar 2027, and the workbooks contain the expected sheets. See [[Features]], [[APIs]], [[Business-Rules]].

- **2026-08-23**: **Fixed the owner Money tab returning 500, and two older breakages it exposed.** `GET /api/owner/payouts/summary` failed on every load. Root cause was not the new code: **migration 070's five `payout_*` columns on `profiles` were never declared on the Prisma `profile` model**, so *every* explicit `select` of them failed at runtime — including `/api/owner/payout-account` and the admin settlement console's payout destination, both shipped months earlier and both broken the whole time. This is the **exact inverse of the 2026-08-22 `navigation` outage** (field declared, column missing → unselected reads break; here column present, field missing → explicit reads break), and the pair is what defines the rule: the danger is the *mismatch*, in either direction. Declared the fields — safe because 070 *is* applied, verified in `information_schema` first — and separately guarded the read model's `bank()` lookup, because the reason a masked four-digit account number could take down an entire money screen was a missing try/catch on the least important field on it. **Migration 075 is now applied** (verified in `information_schema`, then by re-running the exact call that was failing: `degraded: false`). The 075-pending path had been degrading correctly all along, just invisibly behind the 500. `tsc` at its 539 baseline both apps; all checks at baseline. **The backend dev server must be restarted** to pick up the regenerated Prisma client. See [[Bugs]], [[Decisions#ADR-092|ADR-092]].

- **2026-08-23**: **The owner can finally see the payout pipeline — and `gateway_transactions` finally has a writer.** Migration 070 built settlement for the *admin who executes it*; the owner, whose money it is, could see none of it (the only owner-facing route was `/api/owner/payout-account`), and the 2026-08-17 design listed owner-facing views as an explicit non-goal. **Audit first, against live data:** `gateway_transactions` had **0 rows and no writer at all** — the Razorpay webhook routed into `paymentService.handlePaymentWebhook` and never inserted — so the settlement engine had a reader and nothing feeding it, and `payment_attempts` had **0 successful rows**, meaning no gateway payment has ever captured in this environment. Migration 070 *was* applied. **Design:** every rupee is presented in exactly one of four states — Owed / Paid to you directly / With Stayo / In your bank — and the totals are *derived from* their parts (`assembleMonth`) rather than computed separately and validated, so the block reconciles by construction; `settlement_item_transactions.transaction_id` being `UNIQUE` already made "a captured rupee is in exactly one of the last two" structural rather than asserted. A **`FAILED` transfer stays counted inside "With Stayo"** (a total that shrinks when a transfer fails is the fastest way to lose an owner), and rent paid directly to the owner is never summed into anything Stayo owes. **The strip is an event line, not a status line** — "3 tenants paid today · ₹18,500 / in your bank by Wed 27 Aug" — because online rent lands in Stayo's account and stops being an event the owner witnesses, which makes it feel like *less* control than cash. Voice priority **failed › paid today › pending › settled › never** lives in a tested pure function, not JSX ordering, and a failure carries a one-tap route to the payout-account form. Each payout expands into the tenants who paid it with `Stayo fee ₹0` always rendered — an unstated zero reads as a fee somebody chose not to mention. **Promise:** `settlement_items.expected_payout_date` = capture + **2 working days**, computed once and **stored**, because a promise recomputed at read time can never be *missed* — it silently moves; stored, it lets "Last 8 payouts — all on time" be false when it is false. Bank holidays deliberately unmodelled. **Ingestion** writes inside the settlement transaction, guarded by a **Postgres savepoint**: a failed statement aborts the whole transaction, so a plain try/catch would be a lie, and without the savepoint deploying ahead of migration 075 would have failed *every gateway payment in production*. Migration 075's two columns are **deliberately kept out of `schema.prisma`** (the 2026-08-22 outage mechanism), with a promise-less query fallback and `Promise.allSettled` so one unreadable column cannot blank the rest.
